========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I guess… what finally got me to come in is I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. At work, in the warehouse, I’ll be doing inventory counts, and then a beeper goes off or a forklift backs up, or somebody moves a pallet near me, and I’m just pulled right off what I was doing.

And then later I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers in. Which is embarrassing, because I’ve done this kind of work a long time, and I used to be pretty solid with it. The alarms are always going off in there anyway—half the time you can tell which forklift it is just by the sound—but lately it’s like every little thing grabs me and I lose the count.

My sister says I never could leave a storage space alone, but this feels different. Sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, like my body won’t let me just stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like after my shift I should be tired, because moving freight all day isn’t exactly sitting at a desk, but I get home and I’m kind of buzzing.\n\nI’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins out in the garage, and then I find some old thing from years ago and—well, last week I found a box of extension cords and somehow that turned into cleaning half the shelf. My sister says I never could leave a storage space alone, but this feels different. Sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, like my body won’t let me just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic or unusually good in a way that was different from your usual mood, maybe even to the point where others noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Some days I wake up and I’m almost too upbeat, like I can fix everything before dinner—the house, the garage, even stuff at work that isn’t really mine to fix.\n\nAnd then my husband will ask something simple, like whether I’ve thought it through, and I just snap at him. Over nothing, really. I’ll be talking about repainting a room or rearranging the pantry—well, the pantry has needed it for years, don’t get me started—and suddenly I’m angry because he’s not immediately on board. It doesn’t feel like my normal self.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you tell me more about those times when you’ve felt unusually upbeat or energetic—do you feel like your mood is not just good but almost high or elated in a way that’s different from your normal happy mood?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can fix the whole house and the workplace before dinner, but then if my husband questions one of my plans I snap at him over something small.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… “high” is maybe the word, or close to it. It’s not just like I’m in a nice mood because it’s sunny or because we got through a good shift. It’s more like I wake up and feel like I can get everything handled all at once—fix the whole house, straighten out things at work, make all these plans before dinner.

And then if my husband asks one normal question, like “Are you sure?” or “Do we need to do that today?” I get really sharp with him. Like, I snap over something small, and afterward I’m thinking, why did I get so mad? It’s just… it feels bigger than normal happy, I guess.

I hear what you’re asking, like whether it was a solid block of days, not just little bursts. I still don’t know if I can pin it down that neatly. At work, in the warehouse, I’ll be doing inventory counts, and then a beeper goes off or a forklift backs up, or somebody moves a pallet near me, and I’m just pulled right off what I was doing.\n\nAnd then later I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers in. Which is embarrassing, because I’ve done this kind of work a long time, and I used to be pretty solid with it. The alarms are always going off in there anyway—half the time you can tell which forklift it is just by the sound—but lately it’s like every little thing grabs me and I lose the count.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting on impulse or feeling restless, like you can't sit still, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess that’s the word. Like after my shift I should be tired, because moving freight all day isn’t exactly sitting at a desk, but I get home and I’m kind of buzzing.\n\nI’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins out in the garage, and then I find some old thing from years ago and—well, last week I found a box of extension cords and somehow that turned into cleaning half the shelf. My sister says I never could leave a storage space alone, but this feels different. Sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, like my body won’t let me just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic or unusually good in a way that was different from your usual mood, maybe even to the point where others noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Some days I wake up and I’m almost too upbeat, like I can fix everything before dinner—the house, the garage, even stuff at work that isn’t really mine to fix.\n\nAnd then my husband will ask something simple, like whether I’ve thought it through, and I just snap at him. Over nothing, really. I’ll be talking about repainting a room or rearranging the pantry—well, the pantry has needed it for years, don’t get me started—and suddenly I’m angry because he’s not immediately on board. It doesn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you’ve felt unusually upbeat or energetic—do you feel like your mood is not just good but almost high or elated in a way that’s different from your normal happy mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “high” is maybe the word, or close to it. It’s not just like I’m in a nice mood because it’s sunny or because we got through a good shift. It’s more like I wake up and feel like I can get everything handled all at once—fix the whole house, straighten out things at work, make all these plans before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks one normal question, like “Are you sure?” or “Do we need to do that today?” I get really sharp with him. Like, I snap over something small, and afterward I’m thinking, why did I get so mad? It’s just… it feels bigger than normal happy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how often these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic occur and how long they typically last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… maybe about a month? Since my husband stopped working outside the home, really. That changed our whole routine more than I thought it would.\n\nI mean, before that, mornings and evenings had a rhythm—he’d be gone, I’d go to work, come back, do my own thing. Now he’s around more, and I notice these energetic spells happening in that time since everything shifted. It’s not like years and years that I’ve been tracking, but this past month it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic usually last? Do they go away after a few hours, last through the whole day, or go on for several days?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like by the hour. I just know this has been happening over the last month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got thrown off.\n\nLike, before, I had my way of doing things, and now he’s around more, which isn’t bad, it’s just different… and that’s when I started noticing these upbeat, revved-up times more. So I don’t know if I’d say a few hours versus days exactly—it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, my husband has definitely noticed, because some days I get this idea in my head like, oh, I can fix the whole house, and then somehow also straighten things out at work before dinner, like that’s a normal amount of things to take on.\n\nAnd then he’ll ask something simple, like, “Do you really want to start that right now?” and I’ll just snap at him. Over nothing, really. Which isn’t… I mean, I can be stressed like anybody, but this feels sharper, more like I’m too revved up and then irritated the second someone slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah… like I was saying, there are days where I get almost too upbeat, like I’m looking around thinking, “I can fix the whole house today,” and then somehow I’m also going to sort out everything at work before dinner. Which, when I say it out loud, sounds ridiculous, but in the moment it feels completely doable.\n\nAnd my husband will notice because he’ll just ask one little question, like, “Are you sure you want to start that now?” and I get snappy with him. Over something small. Then it turns into me being irritated because he’s slowing me down, when really he’s probably just trying to be practical. That’s not really how I usually want to act.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it exactly as “four days in a row,” like I didn’t write it down or anything. It doesn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you’ve felt unusually upbeat or energetic—do you feel like your mood is not just good but almost high or elated in a way that’s different from your normal happy mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “high” is maybe the word, or close to it. It’s not just like I’m in a nice mood because it’s sunny or because we got through a good shift. It’s more like I wake up and feel like I can get everything handled all at once—fix the whole house, straighten out things at work, make all these plans before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks one normal question, like “Are you sure?” or “Do we need to do that today?” I get really sharp with him. Like, I snap over something small, and afterward I’m thinking, why did I get so mad? It’s just… it feels bigger than normal happy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how often these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic occur and how long they typically last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… maybe about a month? Since my husband stopped working outside the home, really. That changed our whole routine more than I thought it would.\n\nI mean, before that, mornings and evenings had a rhythm—he’d be gone, I’d go to work, come back, do my own thing. Now he’s around more, and I notice these energetic spells happening in that time since everything shifted. It’s not like years and years that I’ve been tracking, but this past month it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic usually last? Do they go away after a few hours, last through the whole day, or go on for several days?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like by the hour. I just know this has been happening over the last month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got thrown off.\n\nLike, before, I had my way of doing things, and now he’s around more, which isn’t bad, it’s just different… and that’s when I started noticing these upbeat, revved-up times more. So I don’t know if I’d say a few hours versus days exactly—it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, my husband has definitely noticed, because some days I get this idea in my head like, oh, I can fix the whole house, and then somehow also straighten things out at work before dinner, like that’s a normal amount of things to take on.\n\nAnd then he’ll ask something simple, like, “Do you really want to start that right now?” and I’ll just snap at him. Over nothing, really. Which isn’t… I mean, I can be stressed like anybody, but this feels sharper, more like I’m too revved up and then irritated the second someone slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah… like I was saying, there are days where I get almost too upbeat, like I’m looking around thinking, “I can fix the whole house today,” and then somehow I’m also going to sort out everything at work before dinner. That’s not really how I usually want to act.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it exactly as “four days in a row,” like I didn’t write it down or anything. Sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, like my body won’t let me just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic or unusually good in a way that was different from your usual mood, maybe even to the point where others noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Some days I wake up and I’m almost too upbeat, like I can fix everything before dinner—the house, the garage, even stuff at work that isn’t really mine to fix.\n\nAnd then my husband will ask something simple, like whether I’ve thought it through, and I just snap at him. Over nothing, really. I’ll be talking about repainting a room or rearranging the pantry—well, the pantry has needed it for years, don’t get me started—and suddenly I’m angry because he’s not immediately on board. It doesn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you’ve felt unusually upbeat or energetic—do you feel like your mood is not just good but almost high or elated in a way that’s different from your normal happy mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “high” is maybe the word, or close to it. It’s not just like I’m in a nice mood because it’s sunny or because we got through a good shift. It’s more like I wake up and feel like I can get everything handled all at once—fix the whole house, straighten out things at work, make all these plans before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks one normal question, like “Are you sure?” or “Do we need to do that today?” I get really sharp with him. Like, I snap over something small, and afterward I’m thinking, why did I get so mad? It’s just… it feels bigger than normal happy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how often these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic occur and how long they typically last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… maybe about a month? Since my husband stopped working outside the home, really. That changed our whole routine more than I thought it would.\n\nI mean, before that, mornings and evenings had a rhythm—he’d be gone, I’d go to work, come back, do my own thing. Now he’s around more, and I notice these energetic spells happening in that time since everything shifted. It’s not like years and years that I’ve been tracking, but this past month it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic usually last? Do they go away after a few hours, last through the whole day, or go on for several days?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like by the hour. I just know this has been happening over the last month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got thrown off.\n\nLike, before, I had my way of doing things, and now he’s around more, which isn’t bad, it’s just different… and that’s when I started noticing these upbeat, revved-up times more. So I don’t know if I’d say a few hours versus days exactly—it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, my husband has definitely noticed, because some days I get this idea in my head like, oh, I can fix the whole house, and then somehow also straighten things out at work before dinner, like that’s a normal amount of things to take on.\n\nAnd then he’ll ask something simple, like, “Do you really want to start that right now?” and I’ll just snap at him. Over nothing, really. Which isn’t… I mean, I can be stressed like anybody, but this feels sharper, more like I’m too revved up and then irritated the second someone slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah… like I was saying, there are days where I get almost too upbeat, like I’m looking around thinking, “I can fix the whole house today,” and then somehow I’m also going to sort out everything at work before dinner. Which, when I say it out loud, sounds ridiculous, but in the moment it feels completely doable.\n\nAnd my husband will notice because he’ll just ask one little question, like, “Are you sure you want to start that now?” and I get snappy with him. Over something small. Then it turns into me being irritated because he’s slowing me down, when really he’s probably just trying to be practical. That’s not really how I usually want to act.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it exactly as “four days in a row,” like I didn’t write it down or anything. It’s more that this whole thing has been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed.\n\nThat kind of threw everything off, you know? Like when someone’s suddenly in the house during the day, even little things feel different. So I’ve noticed these revved-up periods during this past month, but I’m not totally sure how to count them day by day.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was a time when you felt this upbeat or revved-up mood most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean, trying to think of it as four days specifically is hard for me. I keep coming back to the fact that it’s been about a month of this, ever since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got changed.\n\nIt’s like, once he was around more during the day, everything felt different, even silly things like where stuff is left in the kitchen or when I do my chores. So yes, there have been stretches where I’ve felt revved up, but I can’t honestly say I’ve tracked whether it was most of the day for exactly four days in a row.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific stretch of time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row—like a full week day after day, not just off and on or for a few hours at a time?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, like whether it was a solid block of days, not just little bursts. I still don’t know if I can pin it down that neatly. What I can say is this has been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our usual routine got all shifted around.\n\nIt’s funny how that kind of thing changes the whole feel of the house—like when someone’s just there at times they didn’t used to be, you start noticing every little difference. So I’ve had this revved-up, upbeat feeling during that stretch, but I can’t honestly point to one exact week and say, yes, every day all day.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row — like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to get me to say yes or no on the four days, and I’m sorry, I keep circling around it. That’s when I started noticing I felt more revved up. But as far as saying it was constant every day for four days straight… I don’t know that I can pin it down that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to a time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row—like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Right, I know I keep not giving you the clean four-day answer. It’s just hard for me to sort it that way.\n\nWhat I can say is this whole revved-up feeling has been going on about a month, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our routine changed. That’s when everything started feeling different day to day. So it’s not like I can point to one neat stretch and say, “there, those exact four days,” but it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to a time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row — like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: I know, I know, you’re asking if it was four days straight like that, and I keep trying to line it up in my head. I’m sorry, I know you’re asking for the four straight days part. I keep getting stuck on that. What I can say is it’s been going on about a month, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our regular routine got all changed around.

Can you tell me what’s been troubling you or what brings you here today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally got me to come in is I just can’t keep my mind on one thing anymore. At work, in the warehouse, I’ll be doing inventory counts, and then a beeper goes off or a forklift backs up, or somebody moves a pallet near me, and I’m just pulled right off what I was doing.\n\nAnd then later I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers in. Which is embarrassing, because I’ve done this kind of work a long time, and I used to be pretty solid with it. The alarms are always going off in there anyway—half the time you can tell which forklift it is just by the sound—but lately it’s like every little thing grabs me and I lose the count.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting on impulse or feeling restless, like you can't sit still, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess that’s the word. Like after my shift I should be tired, because moving freight all day isn’t exactly sitting at a desk, but I get home and I’m kind of buzzing.\n\nI’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins out in the garage, and then I find some old thing from years ago and—well, last week I found a box of extension cords and somehow that turned into cleaning half the shelf. My sister says I never could leave a storage space alone, but this feels different. Sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, like my body won’t let me just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic or unusually good in a way that was different from your usual mood, maybe even to the point where others noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Some days I wake up and I’m almost too upbeat, like I can fix everything before dinner—the house, the garage, even stuff at work that isn’t really mine to fix.\n\nAnd then my husband will ask something simple, like whether I’ve thought it through, and I just snap at him. Over nothing, really. I’ll be talking about repainting a room or rearranging the pantry—well, the pantry has needed it for years, don’t get me started—and suddenly I’m angry because he’s not immediately on board. It doesn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you’ve felt unusually upbeat or energetic—do you feel like your mood is not just good but almost high or elated in a way that’s different from your normal happy mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “high” is maybe the word, or close to it. It’s not just like I’m in a nice mood because it’s sunny or because we got through a good shift. It’s more like I wake up and feel like I can get everything handled all at once—fix the whole house, straighten out things at work, make all these plans before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks one normal question, like “Are you sure?” or “Do we need to do that today?” I get really sharp with him. Like, I snap over something small, and afterward I’m thinking, why did I get so mad? It’s just… it feels bigger than normal happy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how often these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic occur and how long they typically last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… maybe about a month? Since my husband stopped working outside the home, really. That changed our whole routine more than I thought it would.\n\nI mean, before that, mornings and evenings had a rhythm—he’d be gone, I’d go to work, come back, do my own thing. Now he’s around more, and I notice these energetic spells happening in that time since everything shifted. It’s not like years and years that I’ve been tracking, but this past month it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic usually last? Do they go away after a few hours, last through the whole day, or go on for several days?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like by the hour. I just know this has been happening over the last month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got thrown off.\n\nLike, before, I had my way of doing things, and now he’s around more, which isn’t bad, it’s just different… and that’s when I started noticing these upbeat, revved-up times more. So I don’t know if I’d say a few hours versus days exactly—it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, my husband has definitely noticed, because some days I get this idea in my head like, oh, I can fix the whole house, and then somehow also straighten things out at work before dinner, like that’s a normal amount of things to take on.\n\nAnd then he’ll ask something simple, like, “Do you really want to start that right now?” and I’ll just snap at him. Over nothing, really. Which isn’t… I mean, I can be stressed like anybody, but this feels sharper, more like I’m too revved up and then irritated the second someone slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah… like I was saying, there are days where I get almost too upbeat, like I’m looking around thinking, “I can fix the whole house today,” and then somehow I’m also going to sort out everything at work before dinner. Which, when I say it out loud, sounds ridiculous, but in the moment it feels completely doable.\n\nAnd my husband will notice because he’ll just ask one little question, like, “Are you sure you want to start that now?” and I get snappy with him. Over something small. Then it turns into me being irritated because he’s slowing me down, when really he’s probably just trying to be practical. That’s not really how I usually want to act.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it exactly as “four days in a row,” like I didn’t write it down or anything. It’s more that this whole thing has been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed.\n\nThat kind of threw everything off, you know? Like when someone’s suddenly in the house during the day, even little things feel different. So I’ve noticed these revved-up periods during this past month, but I’m not totally sure how to count them day by day.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was a time when you felt this upbeat or revved-up mood most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean, trying to think of it as four days specifically is hard for me. I keep coming back to the fact that it’s been about a month of this, ever since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got changed.\n\nIt’s like, once he was around more during the day, everything felt different, even silly things like where stuff is left in the kitchen or when I do my chores. So yes, there have been stretches where I’ve felt revved up, but I can’t honestly say I’ve tracked whether it was most of the day for exactly four days in a row.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific stretch of time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row—like a full week day after day, not just off and on or for a few hours at a time?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, like whether it was a solid block of days, not just little bursts. I still don’t know if I can pin it down that neatly. What I can say is this has been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our usual routine got all shifted around.\n\nIt’s funny how that kind of thing changes the whole feel of the house—like when someone’s just there at times they didn’t used to be, you start noticing every little difference. So I’ve had this revved-up, upbeat feeling during that stretch, but I can’t honestly point to one exact week and say, yes, every day all day.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row — like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to get me to say yes or no on the four days, and I’m sorry, I keep circling around it. It’s just hard for me to separate it out like that.\n\nWhat I can say is it’s been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our usual routine changed. But as far as saying it was constant every day for four days straight… I don’t know that I can pin it down that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to a time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row—like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Right, I know I keep not giving you the clean four-day answer. Which is embarrassing, because I’ve done this kind of work a long time, and I used to be pretty solid with it. The alarms are always going off in there anyway—half the time you can tell which forklift it is just by the sound—but lately it’s like every little thing grabs me and I lose the count.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting on impulse or feeling restless, like you can't sit still, even when you know you should?\n\nPatient: Yeah… restless, I guess that’s the word. Like after my shift I should be tired, because moving freight all day isn’t exactly sitting at a desk, but I get home and I’m kind of buzzing.\n\nI’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins out in the garage, and then I find some old thing from years ago and—well, last week I found a box of extension cords and somehow that turned into cleaning half the shelf. My sister says I never could leave a storage space alone, but this feels different. Sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, like my body won’t let me just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually energetic or unusually good in a way that was different from your usual mood, maybe even to the point where others noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Some days I wake up and I’m almost too upbeat, like I can fix everything before dinner—the house, the garage, even stuff at work that isn’t really mine to fix.\n\nAnd then my husband will ask something simple, like whether I’ve thought it through, and I just snap at him. Over nothing, really. I’ll be talking about repainting a room or rearranging the pantry—well, the pantry has needed it for years, don’t get me started—and suddenly I’m angry because he’s not immediately on board. It doesn’t feel like my normal self.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you’ve felt unusually upbeat or energetic—do you feel like your mood is not just good but almost high or elated in a way that’s different from your normal happy mood?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “high” is maybe the word, or close to it. It’s not just like I’m in a nice mood because it’s sunny or because we got through a good shift. It’s more like I wake up and feel like I can get everything handled all at once—fix the whole house, straighten out things at work, make all these plans before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks one normal question, like “Are you sure?” or “Do we need to do that today?” I get really sharp with him. Like, I snap over something small, and afterward I’m thinking, why did I get so mad? It’s just… it feels bigger than normal happy, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me how often these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic occur and how long they typically last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… maybe about a month? Since my husband stopped working outside the home, really. That changed our whole routine more than I thought it would.\n\nI mean, before that, mornings and evenings had a rhythm—he’d be gone, I’d go to work, come back, do my own thing. Now he’s around more, and I notice these energetic spells happening in that time since everything shifted. It’s not like years and years that I’ve been tracking, but this past month it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic usually last? Do they go away after a few hours, last through the whole day, or go on for several days?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like by the hour. So I don’t know if I’d say a few hours versus days exactly—it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, my husband has definitely noticed, because some days I get this idea in my head like, oh, I can fix the whole house, and then somehow also straighten things out at work before dinner, like that’s a normal amount of things to take on.\n\nAnd then he’ll ask something simple, like, “Do you really want to start that right now?” and I’ll just snap at him. Over nothing, really. Which isn’t… I mean, I can be stressed like anybody, but this feels sharper, more like I’m too revved up and then irritated the second someone slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah… like I was saying, there are days where I get almost too upbeat, like I’m looking around thinking, “I can fix the whole house today,” and then somehow I’m also going to sort out everything at work before dinner. Which, when I say it out loud, sounds ridiculous, but in the moment it feels completely doable.\n\nAnd my husband will notice because he’ll just ask one little question, like, “Are you sure you want to start that now?” and I get snappy with him. Yeah… I think there have been days like that. Like I’ll wake up and feel almost too upbeat, like I can fix the whole house and get things at work straightened out before dinner, like it all feels possible and I’m already moving in my head.

It’s not like years and years that I’ve been tracking, but this past month it’s been noticeable.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic usually last? Do they go away after a few hours, last through the whole day, or go on for several days?\n\nPatient: It’s hard for me to pin it down like by the hour. I just know this has been happening over the last month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got thrown off.\n\nLike, before, I had my way of doing things, and now he’s around more, which isn’t bad, it’s just different… and that’s when I started noticing these upbeat, revved-up times more. So I don’t know if I’d say a few hours versus days exactly—it’s been part of this past month.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, my husband has definitely noticed, because some days I get this idea in my head like, oh, I can fix the whole house, and then somehow also straighten things out at work before dinner, like that’s a normal amount of things to take on.\n\nAnd then he’ll ask something simple, like, “Do you really want to start that right now?” and I’ll just snap at him. Over nothing, really. Which isn’t… I mean, I can be stressed like anybody, but this feels sharper, more like I’m too revved up and then irritated the second someone slows me down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a time when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, even to the point where other people noticed you were acting out of character?\n\nPatient: Yeah… like I was saying, there are days where I get almost too upbeat, like I’m looking around thinking, “I can fix the whole house today,” and then somehow I’m also going to sort out everything at work before dinner. Which, when I say it out loud, sounds ridiculous, but in the moment it feels completely doable.\n\nAnd my husband will notice because he’ll just ask one little question, like, “Are you sure you want to start that now?” and I get snappy with him. Over something small. Then it turns into me being irritated because he’s slowing me down, when really he’s probably just trying to be practical. That’s not really how I usually want to act.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if any of these periods of feeling unusually upbeat or energetic lasted for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say it exactly as “four days in a row,” like I didn’t write it down or anything. It’s more that this whole thing has been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed.\n\nThat kind of threw everything off, you know? Like when someone’s suddenly in the house during the day, even little things feel different. So I’ve noticed these revved-up periods during this past month, but I’m not totally sure how to count them day by day.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was a time when you felt this upbeat or revved-up mood most of the day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I mean, trying to think of it as four days specifically is hard for me. I keep coming back to the fact that it’s been about a month of this, ever since my husband stopped working outside the home and our whole routine got changed.\n\nIt’s like, once he was around more during the day, everything felt different, even silly things like where stuff is left in the kitchen or when I do my chores. So yes, there have been stretches where I’ve felt revved up, but I can’t honestly say I’ve tracked whether it was most of the day for exactly four days in a row.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any specific stretch of time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, nearly every day, for at least four days in a row—like a full week day after day, not just off and on or for a few hours at a time?\n\nPatient: I hear what you’re asking, like whether it was a solid block of days, not just little bursts. I still don’t know if I can pin it down that neatly. What I can say is this has been going on for about a month now, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our usual routine got all shifted around.\n\nIt’s funny how that kind of thing changes the whole feel of the house—like when someone’s just there at times they didn’t used to be, you start noticing every little difference. So I’ve had this revved-up, upbeat feeling during that stretch, but I can’t honestly point to one exact week and say, yes, every day all day.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row — like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: I know you’re trying to get me to say yes or no on the four days, and I’m sorry, I keep circling around it. That’s when I started noticing I felt more revved up. But as far as saying it was constant every day for four days straight… I don’t know that I can pin it down that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to a time when you felt this revved-up or upbeat mood almost all day, every day, for at least four days in a row—like it was a constant part of each day, not just coming and going?\n\nPatient: Right, I know I keep not giving you the clean four-day answer.
